WYOMING:
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein have applied for a zoning 
map amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map 
Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”).
Section 2. That on October 3, 2022, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission 
conducted a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zone change.
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC.

UDC-22-00313
October 3, 2022
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of a portion of the South Half of Section 27, Township 13 North, Range 67 
West of the 6th Principal Meridian to City CB Community Business, City LI Light Industrial, and City AG 
Agricultural as shown in the attached zone change map with conditions, noting that (with conditions) 
the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from various county zones to various City zones,  
concurrent with an annexation. The assignment of a City zone is necessary to establish the regulation of 
annexed property. The proposal is to zone 111 acres CB Community Business, 973 acres LI Light Industrial, 
and 151 acres AG Agricultural.  
 
The areas proposed to be zoned CB and AG are analogous to the current County zones. The area proposed to 
be zoned LI is associated with a specific project. 
 
The land is currently largely vacant (although some limited residential and agricultural operations  such as 
grazing  may be operating on the site). The impetus behind the annexation and zone change is an 
anticipated light industrial use.

UDC-22-00313
October 3, 2022
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. The property is within the Urban Service Boundary. Development will 
require infrastructure improvements and investment.   
 
Policies in PlanCheyenne encourage industrial development near regional transportation routes 
and on the edges of the community (where they can be buffered from the residential and 
customer-based commercial uses). 
 
While the current future land use map would not support LI zoning, the Comprehensive plan is 
meant to be a guide and is encouraged to be responsive to market demands. It was originally 
thought that this area would be optimal for largely residential development, but there had been 
no true development proposals for the land. The land is within the urban service boundary and 
would be expected to develop within City limits. 
 
A concurrent PlanCheyenne amendment proposes changing the property to Mixed-use 
Employment for future light industrial development.  The proposed zoning establishes LI for a 
portion of the area for immediate development while zoning the remainder as AG as an interim 
holding zone, and bringing forward the CB zone from the County areas currently zoned County 
CB.      
 
The proposed zoning allows for establishment of a new business park with regional connectivity 
to I-25 and U.S. Highway 85. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1, with conditions. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
A majority of the surrounding property is largely vacant with limited use. The future land use 
map and comprehensive plan speculated that this area would develop with largely residential 
uses, but considered a mix of commercial uses possible. These speculations did not have any 
true development proposals behind them. The large acreage of the site would allow for buffers 
to adjacent property and surrounding areas could still develop with largely residential 
development patterns. The proposed zoning of LI only allows limited industrial uses, which can 
be compatible with nearby residential uses. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 2. 
   
 
 

UDC-22-00313
October 3, 2022
3)
The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The property currently has a County zone, but in order to develop within the City, a City zone is 
required. The proposed areas to be zoned CB and AG are analogous to the current County 
zones. The proposed LI zone district requires screening and buffering along street rights-of-ways 
and district boundaries. Typically, Cheyenne LEADS business parks attract large tenants and 
develop in accordance with applicable landscaping and screening standards.    
  
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
Block sizes are not applicable in areas designated mixed-use employment or industrial. Access 
may be limited based on street classification and proximity to the interstate, but specific access 
would be reviewed with plat(s) or site plan(s). Infrastructure (streets and utilities) exist in the 
region, but will need to be extended to effectively service the subject property. A transportation 
network plan submitted with the project demonstrates the ability for the project to meet  
over-arching transportation goals. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The CB zone is intended for regional-serving commercial uses. Large acreage to allow larger 
businesses is appropriate. The CB zone includes design standards. The area proposed to be 
zoned CB by this zone change is close to regional transportation routes and development should 
be able to meet all design standards. 
 
The AG zone is intended to keep land in a natural state, but is also appropriate as a holding zone 
until more specific development proposals are made. This zone district has no design standards. 
The area proposed to be zoned AG is appropriate as a holding zone. 
 
The LI district is intended for controlled manufacturing or intense employment uses 
that may have accessory commercial, service or office uses, but which cannot readily fit into 
other general business or mixed-use areas due to the nature or intensity of operations. 
The uses and building and site designs in this district should not produce impacts beyond the 
property lines. The LI zone should be in areas which have access to regional infrastructure and 
space to provide buffers to neighboring non-industrial uses. There are no design standards in 
the LI zone. The area proposed to be zoned LI meets these expectations. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 5. 
    
 
 

UDC-22-00313
October 3, 2022
6)
The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The specific development proposed in concert with this rezoning is a light industrial facility on 
the area to be zoned LI. Should the proposed development not be executed after approval, 
there are also a number of industrial and commercial uses which would be eligible to be 
established at this location. The shear size of the proposal allows for a wide variety of 
development options. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
Reviewing agencies noted that the concurrent future land use map amendment needs to be 
approved to recommend approval of the zone change. No other recommendations regarding 
the proposed zone change were provided.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
The area proposed to be zoned LI does abut existing residential areas to the east and southeast. 
However, there is anticipated to be roads between the subject property and the residential 
areas, and the size of the property allows for buffering between the LI use of the land and these 
existing residential areas. A Greenway is also located here on the future Greenway Map, and 
may help provide a buffer. City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not 
adversely affect the public health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, reviewing agencies noted that the concurrent future land use map 
amendment needs to be approved to recommend approval of the zone change. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d). The PUD zone is 
appropriate based on the comprehensive plan (as long as the concurrent future land use map 
amendment is approved), surrounding zoning, and surrounding development patterns. 
 

UDC-22-00313
October 3, 2022
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant. 
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications (Staff 
Recommendation).  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If 
ecommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from County CB and County A-2 to City CB, 
City AG, and City LI, with the condition that the concurrent future land use map amendment be 
approved. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-11-01_TotalExtraction_13293483.pdf
ORDINANCE NO. _________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN 
ORDINANCE 
AMENDING 
SECTION 
5.12.110, 
OPEN 
CONTAINER—SALES 
AND 
CONSUMPTION—EXCEPTIONS, 
OF 
CHAPTER 5.12, ALCOHOLIC LIQUOR AND MALT BEVERAGES, OF 
TITLE 5, BUSINESS LICENSES AND REGULATIONS, OF THE 
MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, 
AMENDING PROVISIONS GOVERNING THE POSSESSION OF 
ALCOHOLIC LIQUOR OR MALT BEVERAGES IN AN OPEN 
CONTAINER AS SPECIFIED." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 5.12.110, Open Container—Sales and Consumption—Exceptions, of 
Chapter 5.12 - Alcoholic Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and 
Regulations, of the code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out in this 
ordinance. 
 
5.12.110 - Open container; —Sales and consumption; —Exceptions. 
 
A. Except in compliance with Unless authorized under Wyo. Stat. Sections 12-4-410(e) and 
12-4-413(f) and other provisions of this chapter, including Section 5.12.120, Special use 
areas—Special event permit, or otherwise authorized under this Chapter, including 
subsections (B), (D), (E) and (G) of this this section and Section 5.12.120 of the city code, 
no person shall consume or carry in their immediate possession an alcoholic liquor or malt 
beverage in an open container, either on foot, within a motor vehicle or other means of 
conveyance, upon city streets, sidewalks, parks, public parking lots, public school property, 
or without the consent of the owner upon any private parking lot or any private areas that 
are open to the public., except: 
 
1.  The chief of police may authorize the city clerk to issue a consumption permit to 
applicable applicants requesting to consume or carry an alcoholic liquor or malt 
beverage in an open container on foot or within a motor vehicle or other means of 
conveyance as part of a ceremony, show, theatre, parade, or similar public event. A 
consumption permit does not allow the "sell" or "sale" of alcoholic liquor or malt 
beverages. 
 
2.  The chief of police may authorize the city clerk to issue a consumption permit to 
applicants requesting to consume or carry malt beverages only in parks and facilities 
with spaces which may be individually reserved when approved by the city's 
department director or authorized representative, overseeing the grounds or responsible 
for reserving the grounds for applicant use in accordance with facility reservation 
policies. 
 
3.  The chief of police may authorize the city clerk to issue a consumption permit to 
By John Brodie at 9:47 am, Oct 19, 2022

 
Page 2 of 5 
applicants to consume or carry champagne and/or wine only in parks and facilities with 
spaces which may be individually reserved when approved by the city's department 
director, or authorized representative, overseeing the grounds or responsible for 
reserving the grounds for applicant use in accordance with facility reservation policies. 
 
4.  Prior to consumption permits being issued, an applicant shall provide the city clerk with 
a written release, indemnification and hold harmless agreement and agree to assume 
responsibility relative to liquor liability coverage as approved by the city risk manager 
or city attorney. 
 
B. The city clerk, with approval by the chief of police, may issue a catering or malt beverage 
permit, including a waiver of open container restrictions, to applicants requesting to have 
offered for sale: 
 
1. Applicants requesting to sell aAlcoholic liquor and/or malt beverages at the Cheyenne-
Kiwanis Community House, Cheyenne Botanic Gardens and affiliated facilities, 
located within Lions Park; the community center located within the David R. Romero 
South Cheyenne Community Park; Cheyenne Civic Center; and the city-owned ice and 
events center, when under lease, as authorized by city staff responsible for reserving 
the facility for applicant use in accordance with facility reservation policies;. 
 
2. Applicants requesting to sell mMalt beverages in conjunction with adult 
softball/baseball games and tournaments held at the Pioneer Park Baseball Complex 
and Powers Field Complex, as authorized by city staff responsible for reserving the 
facility for applicant use in accordance with facility reservation policies. Sales provided 
under a catering permit shall be restricted to malt beverages only; or. 
 
3. The chief of police and city clerk may approve catering permits and malt beverage 
permits for sales, and to consume and carry, Alcoholic liquor and/or malt beverages in 
conjunction with events and activities an event or activity held within Frontier Park, 
which permit shall also authorize consumption and carry by patrons of the event or 
activity. 
 
4.C.  Permits issued authorized under Section 5.12.110 of this chapter subsection (B) of this 
section shall not be issued unless: 
 
a.1.  The applicant submits proof of general liability coverage, including liquor liability, as 
approved and in an amount determined by the city risk manager or city attorney. Proof 
of coverage document must name the city of Cheyenne as an additional insured and 
certificate holder for the date and location of the event or activities for which the permit 
will be issued; 
 
b.2.  The applicant provides the city clerk with a written release, indemnification and hold 
harmless agreement as stated upon the permit application form; and 

 
Page 3 of 5 
 
c.3.  Sales, dispensing and consumption boundaries are approved by the chief of police and 
city clerk. 
 
C.D.  The city clerk, with approval by the chief of police, may issue a catering or malt beverage 
permit, with a waiver of open container restrictions, for community-based events and 
activities proposed to be held upon city-owned property, including the Depot Plaza; streets; 
sidewalks; and rights-of-way. A catering permit may be restricted to sales of wine and malt 
beverages only. 
 
E. Subject to the requirements imposed under subsection (F) of this section, the chief of police 
may authorize the city clerk to issue a consumption permit to any applicant requesting to 
consume or carry: 
 
1. An alcoholic liquor or malt beverage in an open container on foot or as a passenger 
within a motor vehicle or other means of conveyance as part of a ceremony, show, 
theatre, parade or similar public event. A consumption permit shall not authorize the 
permit holder to sell alcoholic liquor or malt beverages; or 
 
2. Champagne, wine and/or malt beverages in a park or facility with a space that may be 
individually reserved when approved by the city's department director or authorized 
representative, overseeing the grounds or responsible for reserving the grounds for 
applicant use in accordance with facility reservation policies. A consumption permit 
may be restricted to malt beverages only. 
 
E.F. 
Prior to the city clerk issuing a consumption permit under subsection (E) of this 
section, the permit applicant shall provide the city clerk with a written release and an 
indemnification and hold harmless agreement and agree to assume responsibility relative 
to liquor liability coverage as approved by the city risk manager or city attorney. 
 
F.G. 
Exceptions:. 
 
1. It shall not be unlawful for any person to consume or have in his or her possession any 
alcoholic liquor or malt beverage in a container that has been open or unsealed where 
a state limited transportation liquor license has been issued by the Wyoming Liquor 
Division. While traveling within the city, each vehicle operating under a state limited 
transportation liquor license must have a copy of the license conspicuously displayed 
in the rear window of the vehicle. 
 
2. Alcoholic liquor or malt beverages may be consumed by those who are of legal 
drinking age within a limousine or horse-drawn carriage traveling within the city for 
the purposes of special private activities, as approved by the chief of police, in which 
there is no fee for the alcoholic liquor or malt beverages assessed by the owner or 
operator of the limousine or horse-drawn carriage. 

 
Page 4 of 5 
 
3. The possession of open containers is shall be permitted within the downtown 
development authority's boundary defined in Section 2.76.040 of the city code, between 
the Thursday preceding Memorial Day to the Monday of Labor Day of each calendar 
year. Beginning December 31, 2022, the possession of open containers shall be 
permitted within the downtown development authority's boundary defined in Section 
2.76.040 of the city code year-round. The possession of open containers under this 
paragraph shall be subject to the following conditions: 
 
i.a.  This The possession of open containers shall only be allowed between the 
hours of 8:00 a.m. and 10:00 p.m.; 
 
ii.b.  This section allows paragraph shall only allow for the possession of open 
containers of wine and malt beverages; only. 
 
iii.c.  Only tThe possession of open containers shall only be authorized through the 
use of plastic cups for open container is permitted. Glass containers are or reusable 
containers provided by a wine or malt beverage vendor. The use of any glass 
container shall be strictly prohibited under this paragraph;. 
 
iv.d.  By resolution, the governing body may impose additional restrictions, or may 
modify the restrictions imposed under this subsection, on periods of time, 
boundaries of special areas or districts, beverage distribution, tracking, and or event 
control; may be established by resolution of the governing body. 
 
v.e.  Possession of open containers shall always be allowed on the following 
holidays between the hours of 8:00 a.m. and 10:00 p.m. A wine or malt beverage 
vendor shall refuse to sell or offer for sale wine or malt beverages in an open 
container to any person during an inclement weather event if in judgement of the 
vendor, the health and safety of the person will be endangered due to the effects of 
alcohol on the person and the dangers presented due to inclement weather event; 
and 
 
1.  4th of July; 
 
2.  Cheyenne Day; 
 
3.  Founder's Day or Statehood Day. 
 
f.  The possession of open containers shall only be permitted on foot within the 
downtown development authority's boundary defined in Section 2.76.040 of the 
city code. Open containers in motor vehicles or other means of conveyance shall 
be strictly prohibited. 
 

 
Page 5 of 5 
 
Section 2.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable.  If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases.  
 
Section 3.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and 
publication.

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM P PUBLIC TO CB COMMUNITY BUSINESS FOR LAND 
LOCATED SOUTH OF MISSILE DRIVE, EAST OF I-25.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING:
Section 1.
That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1,
Zoning Map Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”).
Section 2.
That on November 7, 2022, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission 
conducted a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zone change.
Section 3.
That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC.
Section 4.
That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 
5.1.2, Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property 
described as Lot 1, Block 1, Pat Griffin Park, Second Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming, is hereby changed to CB Community Business.
Section 5.
That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend 
the Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change 
as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7
below.
Section 6.
Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases.
Section 7.
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and
publication.

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION

STAFF REPORT
FILE:
UDC-22-00354: Car Dealership Zoning Map Amendment
REQUEST:
A request to rezone approximately 1.5 acres of land from P Public to CB 
Community Business
LOCATION:
South of the I-25 and Missile Drive interchange
APPLICANT:
Gene MacDonald
AGENT:
Shane Hansen
OWNER:
Moreland Properties LLC
ZONING:
P Public
FUTURE LAND USE:
Mixed-use Commercial
PURPOSE:
To allow non-governmental development
PREPARED BY:
Seth Lloyd, Planner II - Development
Planning and Development Department
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

UDC-22-00354
November 7, 2022
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lot 1, Block 1, Pat Griffin Park Second Filing to CB Community Business 
as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for 
approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from P Public to CB Community Business for 1.5 
acres of land.  
 
The proposed CB zoning allows variety of commercial uses in an automobile-centric format. 
 
The land was once under City ownership and as part of the negotiations to transfer the land out of City 
ownership, it was understood that the new owner would be required to do a zone change out of the P Public 
zone to develop the property. P Public zoning cannot develop under private ownership. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
existing infrastructure. 
 
Future Land Use Designation  
Zoning District 
Land Use 
Subject 
Site 
Mixed-use Commercial  
P Public 
Vacant 
North 
Mixed-use Commercial, 
unassigned
CB Community Business 
Vacant, Interchange 
South 
Mixed-use Commercial 
P Public 
Vacant (creek) 
East 
Mixed-use Commercial     
CB 
Vacant 
West

SS 
Vacant (EUL) 

UDC-22-00354
November 7, 2022
The location associated with a freeway interchange would encourage the development of 
automobile-oriented commercial uses in this area. PlanCheyenne encourages the flexibility to be 
responsive to market demands. 
 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property and the surrounding area is Mixed-use 
Commercial. 
future land use designation is MUB Mixed-use Business Emphasis, although a variety of 
commercial, residential, and mixed-use zones could also be appropriate based on the context of 
the site. The proposed zoning is CB, a commercial-emphasis zone. Mixed-use Commercial areas 
are encouraged to develop with primarily commercial uses, but public, residential, and other 
employment uses are also encouraged. 
 
This property is also near a Regional Activity Center on the Future Land Use Map. Activity 
Centers are intended for a mix of commercial and residential uses with access to regional 
transportation. Regional-serving commercial uses is an expected component of an Activity 
Center. The proposed CB zone is considered one of the optimal zones for activity centers of this 
type.  
 
The policies of PlanCheyenne are supportive of this proposed zone change. The future land use 
plan encourages more commercial-style zoning at this location.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
The only adjacent privately-owned property is zoned CB. Zoning the subject property CB would 
allow the properties to be consolidated into one development. The proposed zoning is also 
compatible with the future land use designation of Mixed-use Commercial and an activity 
center.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The P zone is only eligible to develop with governmental uses. As the property is owned by a 
private entity, a non-P zone designation is required to allow the property to develop. CB zoning 
allows a variety of commercial uses (and limited residential) and is expected to develop with an 
automobile-oriented development pattern.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
 
 

UDC-22-00354
November 7, 2022
4)
The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
Due to the constraints presented by I-25 and the creek bed, it is unlikely this property can meet 
the block standards of Article 4 for any proposed development. Exceptions for such features are 
written into article four. The property is adjacent to an open area which may include a future 
Greenway. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The CB zone is intended to develop with regional-serving retail, employment, service, and civic 
uses in activity centers or areas designated Community Business by the comprehensive plan. 
This area is designated as an activity center by PlanCheyenne and has ready access to major 
transportation routes, including I-25. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The specific development proposed in concert with this rezoning is a car dealership (outdoor 
sales). The property should be combined with the property to the east to create one large, 
commercially developable parcel. The larger parcel should have no issues meeting standards for 
CB-style development. Should the proposed development not be executed after approval, there 
are a number of commercial uses which would be eligible to be established at this location. 
Multifamily residential uses could also be appropriate. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
 

UDC-22-00354
November 7, 2022
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no review comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d). The CB zone is 
appropriate based on the comprehensive plan and context of the property. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from P Public to CB Community Business. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-11-01_TotalExtraction_72000353.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. _____________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE FORMATION OF THE WISE 
WATER 
ACTION 
COMMITTEE 
TO 
DEVELOP 
A 
WATER 
CONSERVATION 
ROADMAP 
SPECIFIC 
TO 
CHEYENNE 
AND 
SUPPORTED BY CITY DECISION MAKERS AND COMMUNITY 
STAKEHOLDERS." 
 
WHEREAS, City leaders have always looked to the future to secure a reliable water supply 
for Cheyenne; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Western United States is currently facing unprecedented drought 
conditions with short-term and long-term impacts to the City of Cheyenne, including possible 
Colorado River curtailment; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities (BOPU) manages a complex 
water supply system and delivers water to meet Cheyenne’s demands. That system is finite; 
 
 
WHEREAS, future community growth depends on careful stewardship of our water 
resources in cooperation with public and private entities; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the formation of the Wise Water Action Committee (WWAC) will allow for 
a review of best practices and identify those most practical to develop a water conservation 
roadmap to ensure a sustainable future for Cheyenne. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, the WWAC shall be comprised of representatives from BOPU, 
the Department of Community Recreation and Events, Department of Planning and Development, 
and members of the City Council, which representatives will regularly meet and provide clear 
communication and direction for all water stakeholders; and 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the WWAC will look to City leaders for support of 
their recommendations to implement water conservation policies and programs in Cheyenne. 
 
PRESENTED, READ, AND ADOPTED this _____ day of ___________________, 2022. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor 
 
(Seal) 
 
ATTEST: 
 
__________________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
By John Brodie at 9:30 am, Oct 05, 2022


--- END OF FILE ---

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION PERMITTING THE POSSESSION OF 
OPEN 
CONTAINERS WITHIN THE DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT DISTRICT 
BOUNDARIES FOR POST-THANKSGIVING SHOPPING AND THE 
CHRISTMAS PARADE TO BE HELD ON NOVEMBER 25 & 26, 2022." 
 
WHEREAS, Section 5.12.110(D) of the Municipal Code permits the possession of open 
containers of wine and malt beverages within the boundaries of the downtown development district 
between the hours of 8:00 a.m. and 10:00 p.m. from the Thursday preceding Memorial Day to the 
Monday of Labor Day of each calendar year; 
 
WHEREAS, under Section 5.12.110(D), the governing body may modify by resolution the 
restrictions imposed on the periods of time and the boundaries of special areas or districts; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority requests the possession of 
open containers of wine and malt beverages within the boundaries of the downtown development 
district be permitted for the purpose of Post-Thanksgiving Shopping and the Christmas Parade on 
both Friday, November 25, 2022 and Saturday, November 26, 2022, from 8 a.m. to 10:00 p.m.; 
 
 
WHEREAS, shopping locally creates a multiplier effect through direct, indirect and 
induced spending which boosts the local economy and creates more local wealth and jobs;  
 
 
WHEREAS, a number of studies illustrated on the website of American Independence 
Business Alliance have shown a forty-eight percent (48%) return to the community occurs when 
consumers spend locally; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the event organizers will comply with any and all laws, rules and regulations 
to ensure the safety of the community;  
 
 
WHEREAS, adherence to the following requirements shall be required for participation by 
alcohol vendors and consumers when open containers are permitted as designated herein: 
 
1. 
The only containers authorized for alcoholic beverages in the special district 
downtown area’s public spaces, for the day specified herein, shall be plastic cups. Glass containers 
are strictly prohibited.   
 
 
2. 
No outside alcoholic beverage, wine or malt liquor may be brought into or 
consumed within the open container area and any violation of this provision shall constitute a 
violation of the City’s open container prohibition and may incur additional civil or criminal 
liability. 
 
 
3. 
Nothing herein shall be interpreted to authorize the possession of open containers 
of alcoholic liquor or malt beverages in or on motor vehicles. 
 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 2:04 am, Oct 17, 2022

 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, the possession of open containers of wine and malt beverages 
within the boundaries of the downtown development district shall be permitted for Post-
Thanksgiving Shopping and the Christmas, during the dates and times designated herein. 
 
 
PRESENTED, READ, AND ADOPTED this ______ day of ________________, 2022. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
__________________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Patrick Collins, Mayor

WHEREAS, Section 5.12.110(D) of the Municipal Code permits the possession of open 
containers of wine and malt beverages within the boundaries of the downtown development district 
between the hours of 8:00 a.m. and 10:00 p.m. from the Thursday preceding Memorial Day to the

WHEREAS, under Section 5.12.110(D), the governing body may modify by resolution the 
restrictions imposed on the periods of time and the boundaries of special areas or districts; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority requests the possession of 
open containers of wine and malt beverages within the boundaries of the downtown development 
district be permitted for the purpose of the “Santa 0.5K” to be held on Saturday, December 3, 2022, 
from 1:00 p.m. to 2:00 p.m.; 
 
 
WHEREAS, shopping locally creates a multiplier effect through direct, indirect and 
induced spending which boosts the local economy and creates more local wealth and jobs;  
 
 
WHEREAS, a number of studies illustrated on the website of American Independence 
Business Alliance have shown a forty-eight percent (48%) return to the community occurs when 
consumers spend locally; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the event organizers will comply with any and all laws, rules and regulations 
to ensure the safety of the community;  
 
 
WHEREAS, adherence to the following requirements shall be required for participation by 
alcohol vendors and consumers when open containers are permitted as designated herein: 
 
1. 
The only containers authorized for alcoholic beverages in the special district 
downtown area’s public spaces, for the day specified herein, shall be plastic cups. Glass containers 
are strictly prohibited.   
 
 
2. 
No outside alcoholic beverage, wine or malt liquor may be brought into or 
consumed within the open container area and any violation of this provision shall constitute a 
violation of the City’s open container prohibition and may incur additional civil or criminal 
liability. 
 
 
3. 
Nothing herein shall be interpreted to authorize the possession of open containers 
of alcoholic liquor or malt beverages in or on motor vehicles. 
 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 2:03 am, Oct 17, 2022

 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, the possession of open containers of wine and malt beverages 
within the boundaries of the downtown development district shall be permitted for the “Santa 
0.5K”, during the dates and times designated herein. 
 
 
PRESENTED, READ, AND ADOPTED this ______ day of ________________, 2022. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
__________________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Patrick Collins, Mayor 
(SEAL) 
Attest: 
 
__________________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-11-01_TotalExtraction_50247193.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION PERMITTING THE POSSESSION OF 
OPEN 
CONTAINERS WITHIN THE DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT DISTRICT 
BOUNDARIES FOR NEW YEAR’S EVE ON DECEMBER 31, 2022." 
 
WHEREAS, Section 5.12.110(D) of the Municipal Code permits the possession of open 
containers of wine and malt beverages within the boundaries of the downtown development district 
between the hours of 8:00 a.m. and 10:00 p.m. from the Thursday preceding Memorial Day to the 
Monday of Labor Day of each calendar year; 
 
WHEREAS, under Section 5.12.110(D), the governing body may modify by resolution the 
restrictions imposed on the periods of time and the boundaries of special areas or districts; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority requests the possession of 
open containers of wine and malt beverages within the boundaries of the downtown development 
district be permitted for the purpose of New Year’s Eve on Saturday, December 31, 2022, from 
12:00 p.m. until January 1, 2023 at 2:00 a.m.; 
 
 
WHEREAS, shopping locally creates a multiplier effect through direct, indirect and 
induced spending which boosts the local economy and creates more local wealth and jobs;  
 
 
WHEREAS, a number of studies illustrated on the website of American Independence 
Business Alliance have shown a forty-eight percent (48%) return to the community occurs when 
consumers spend locally; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the event organizers will comply with any and all laws, rules and regulations 
to ensure the safety of the community;  
 
 
WHEREAS, adherence to the following requirements shall be required for participation by 
alcohol vendors and consumers when open containers are permitted as designated herein: 
 
1. 
The only containers authorized for alcoholic beverages in the special district 
downtown area’s public spaces, for the day specified herein, shall be plastic cups. Glass containers 
are strictly prohibited.   
 
 
2. 
No outside alcoholic beverage, wine or malt liquor may be brought into or 
consumed within the open container area and any violation of this provision shall constitute a 
violation of the City’s open container prohibition and may incur additional civil or criminal 
liability. 
 
 
3. 
Nothing herein shall be interpreted to authorize the possession of open containers 
of alcoholic liquor or malt beverages in or on motor vehicles. 
 
 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 2:01 am, Oct 17, 2022

 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, the possession of open containers of wine and malt beverages 
within the boundaries of the downtown development district shall be permitted for New Year’s 
Eve, during the dates and times designated herein. 
 
 
 
PRESENTED, READ, AND ADOPTED this ______ day of ________________, 2022. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
__________________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Patrick Collins, Mayor

Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

AND SOUTH GREELEY HIGHWAY, SOUTH OF COLLEGE 
DRIVE).” 
 
 
WHEREAS, Cheyenne LEADS and Goat Systems, LLC have subdivided said land in 
accordance with the statutes in such cases made and provided; 
 
 
WHEREAS, Cheyenne LEADS and Goat Systems, LLC the owners of the above

particularly describing the lots, block, easements, and right-of-way; and,

STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE:

REQUEST:

145-acre lot under AG zoning for future development of an unknown type

Drive 
AGENT: 
AVI, PC – Brad Emmons 
APPLICANT: 
Cheyenne Leads

Michael James Sara; David and Elizabeth Schroth; Sara, William A and 
Ginger L Joint Revocable Trust; Goat Systems LLC; Swan Ranch LLC

2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282

UDC-22-00312  
 
 
October 3, 2022 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the High Plains Business Park Expedited Preliminary Plat advancing the Final Plat to 
the Governing Body, with the staff conditions as listed in the Staff report. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval 
• 
UDC 2.1.3 Final Plat Approval  
• 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
• 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a preliminary plat for 1,123.6 acres of land. The subject property is 
unplatted, largely vacant land, which is concurrently being annexed to the City.   
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Preliminary Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d  – a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
The Future Land Use Map found in the Comprehensive Plan has a pending request for a change 
to Mixed-use Employment for the subject property. Upon approval of the concurrent 
application, the proposed subdivision conforms to PlanCheyenne’s Land Use Plan.

UDC-22-00312  
 
 
October 3, 2022 
Foundation 1 of PlanCheyenne encourages community growth. This development is in response 
to market demands and is within the Urban Service Boundary. The development patterns 
proposed by this plat are compatible with the designations found on the future land use map. 
 
Foundation 3 encourages the recruitment of employers to provided family-wage jobs (3.1.b) and 
to promote a business-friendly environment (3.1.c).     
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.  
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
This plat facilitates development of a business park. Rights-of-way have been provided around 
the plat boundary to facilitate connections to nearby undeveloped property. The plat reflects 
sound urban design principles. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Community Facility Fees (UDC 4.2): 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. 
Lands reserved as unbuildable (for right-of-way, detention, parks, etc), are exempt from 
fees. Also, land zoned AG is exempt until rezoned. Fees based on 949.3 acres of land (Lot 1): 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$332,255 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee  
 
 
 
$332,255 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$664,510.00 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
Commercial Enhancement Fee $1,100 per acre 
 
Transportation Network (UDC 4.3): 
The applicant provided a transportation network plan for the area surrounding the subject 
property. Due to the size of Lot 1, the originally-developed plan for the street network (as 
most recently adopted by Connect 2045) needs to be modified. The network plan associated 
with this submittal informs the concurrent Comprehensive Plan amendment. 
 
The block sizes proposed do not meet standards, however, block sizes are not applicable in 
areas zoned LI or designated Mixed-use Employment by the future land use map – if the 
associated Future Land Use Map amendment is adopted, the blocks proposed by this plat 
would be acceptable.   
 
 
 

UDC-22-00312  
 
 
October 3, 2022 
Open Space (UDC 4.4): 
This proposal does not meet the open space requirement for areas designated Urban 
Residential on the Future Land Use Map. Open Space is required at the site plan level for  
areas designated mixed-use employment by the future land use map; however, LI zoning is 
not required to provide open space. 
 
The future Greenway plan shows a Greenway running along the eastern side of this 
proposed plat area. A Greenway easement should be provided to allow for this planned 
Greenway. 
 
Required Improvements (UDC 4.5): 
This plat appears to be able to provide all required utilities to the lots within this 
development.  
 
Public Safety Fees (UDC 4.6): 
Public Safety Fees are required. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public Safety Fees are payable at 
the issuance of a building permit for each structure. This fee is subject to change annually.    
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No phasing is specifically proposed with this project. However, due to the presence of AG zoning 
on the western parcel, it can be inferred that the western parcel would develop later. 
Infrastructure is located closer to the property on the east, so developing the eastern parcel first 
is logical. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
A drainage study was submitted and reviewed.  
 
Traffic: 
A traffic study was submitted and reviewed. There are multiple corrections to the study 
requested, but these corrections would not change the plat proposal, and the issues identified 
will be resolved with the Engineering Plan Review or Site Plan for the specific development of 
the parcel. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 

UDC-22-00312  
 
 
October 3, 2022 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
The proposed plat proposes 2 buildable lots. The western lot will need to be rezoned before any 
non-agricultural development occurs on it. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
The plat does not propose any unbuildable lots.    
 
Review criterion not applicable.  
  
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
This plat proposes right-of-way along most of the edges, which would allow neighboring 
properties to tie in wherever appropriate. Most of the surrounding properties are undeveloped. 
To the east and southeast, there is existing residential development. However, to both the south 
and southeast, the plat provides streets on the edge of the plat. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, 
or general welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this plat. 
 
Review criterion not applicable. 
 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. Staff has recommended conditions to be reflected on the Final Plat prior to City 
Council approval.      

UDC-22-00312  
 
 
October 3, 2022 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Staff has recommended several conditions to the plat to bring it into further conformance with 
the standards of the code.    
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
   
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.    
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Greenway plan, easements, and facilities. 
2. Technical issues with the plat map. 
3. Technical issues with the Traffic Study. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the expedited plat application with conditions.  
 
The general layout of the plat is compatible with the proposed Comprehensive Plan amendment, with 
the proposed zoning, and with surrounding development patterns. Easements need to be added, and 
some technical corrections or clarifications need to be provided.   
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body.  
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body (Staff 
Recommendation).  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  

UDC-22-00312  
 
 
October 3, 2022 
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to 
staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons 
(based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
Staff recommends approval of the expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body, with the following conditions: 
1. The concurrent comprehensive plan amendment be approved. 
2. Prior to approval of the final plat the final plat map shall be modified as follows: 
a. Technical corrections to the plat map. 
b. Add Greenway easement consistent with the Greenway plan. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Zoning Map  
2. Land Use Plan Map 
3. Master Application Form 
4. Preliminary Plat 
5. Final Plat

ENTITLED: “A 
RESOLUTION CERTIFYING THE 
KIWANIS 
(EAST) 
PARK 
GREENWAY UNDERPASS AND DRAINAGE PLAN AT THE UNION 
PACIFIC RAILROAD.” 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne purchased the Kiwanis Park land in 2020 with sixth-
penny Optional Sales and Use Tax for the development of a new regional park in eastern Cheyenne 
located in the southwest corner of East Pershing Boulevard and Whitney Road; 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne has developed a Master Plan for the Kiwanis Park; 
 
WHEREAS, the east side of Cheyenne continues to grow with new neighborhoods and 
industrial and commercial employment centers;  
 
WHEREAS, The Greater Cheyenne Greenway has grown to over 45 miles and would be 
extended into the Kiwanis Park and is being extended into the Cheyenne LEADS Business Park 
south of the Kiwanis Park; 
 
WHEREAS, the Laramie County Conservation District (LCCD) has move to and opened 
their headquarters in the Cheyenne LEADS Business Park southeast of the Kiwanis Park and 
UPRR Mainline tracks where Dry Creek flows through; 
 
WHEREAS, the Dry Creek Drainage goes through the Kiwanis Park and crosses under 
the Union Pacific Mainline Railroad track and then flows into the Cheyenne LEADS Business 
Park; 
 
WHEREAS, the Dry Creek Drainage has a history of flooding against the embankment of 
the UPRR Mainline track as it did during the historic 1985 Cheyenne Flood; 
 
WHEREAS, the U.S. Department of Transportation FAST Act added to the Metropolitan 
Planning Factors to improve the resiliency and reliability of the transportation system and reduce 
or mitigate stormwater impacts of surface transportation; 
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Metropolitan Planning Organization (MPO) retained Summit 
Engineering September 28, 2020 to develop the Kiwanis (East) Park Greenway Underpass And 
Drainage Plan; 
 
WHEREAS, the Kiwanis (East) Park Greenway Underpass And Drainage Plan explored 
the potential for reducing the buildup of stormwater along the north side of the Union Pacific 
Railroad Embankment and improve flood mitigation for surrounding properties; 
 
WHEREAS, the Kiwanis (East) Park Greenway Underpass And Drainage Plan identified 
a route for the Greater Cheyenne Greenway through Kiwanis Park and across the Union Pacific 
Railroad (UPRR) to join with the planned LEADS Trail along Campstool Road; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Kiwanis (East) Park Greenway Underpass And Drainage Plan has 
developed a creative concept to redirect the drainage of Dry Creek under the UPRR Mainline

track so that the existing drainage structure can be repurposed into a Greenway and pedestrian 
tunnel connecting the Kiwanis Park with the LCCD property in the Cheyenne LEADS Business

WHEREAS, the proposed drainage upgrade will greatly improve the resilience and 
reliability of the nation’s main cross-country rail freight corridor by protecting it from a potential 
flood water breach as what nearly happened in the Cheyenne 1985 Flood; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne has obtained Federal Emergency Management

East Park Greenway Underpass & Drainage Plan at the Union Pacific Railroad 
2 
 
Executive Summary 
Purpose 
The City of Cheyenne continues to expand eastward.  As the City expands so should the recreational 
amenities.  The City purchased the East Park land in 2020 with sixth-penny sales tax funds approved by 
Laramie County voters.  In July of 2022 the park was renamed Kiwanis Park.  The purpose of this study is 
two-fold: 
• 
Identify a route for the Greater Cheyenne Greenway through Kiwanis Park and across the Union 
Pacific Railroad (UPRR) to join with the planned LEADS Trail along Campstool Road. 
• 
Explore the potential for reducing the buildup of stormwater along the north side of the Union 
Pacific Railroad Embankment and improve flood mitigation for surrounding properties. 
The Kiwanis Park land plays a significant role in the Lower Dry Creek Drainage Basin as it serves as a 
detention storage area.  Runoff is detained until it is able to pass through the Union Pacific Railroad 
Embankment via a masonry arch structure constructed for this purpose in 1903.  The figure below shows 
the extent of the FEMA floodplain and floodway in this area.  
 
Figure 1: FEMA Floodway and Floodplain at Kiwanis Park 

 
East Park Greenway Underpass & Drainage Plan at the Union Pacific Railroad 
3 
 
Considerations 
Providing a route for the Greenway across the UPRR tracks requires either a pedestrian overpass or a 
pedestrian underpass.  If the sole purpose of this project was to provide a Greenway crossing then both 
an overpass option and an underpass option would be equally considered.  However, the purpose of this 
study is to also identify ways to reduce the floodway footprint and reduce the buildup of stormwater 
along the UPRR embankment.  Impoundment of storm water against this embankment, which was 
neither designed nor constructed with such impoundment in mind, is a safety concern should the 
embankment fail during a significant storm event. 
Additionally, this land holds a significant interest to history and railroad enthusiasts as the original 
transcontinental rail bed was constructed here in the 1870’s as part of the Pacific Railroad Acts of 1862.  
Remnants of this original embankment are located inside Kiwanis Park. 
Explored Alternatives  
The alternatives explored were predicated on two 
main factors:  
• 
Incorporating the existing remnants of 
the transcontinental rail bed into a berm 
to serve as the downstream side of the 
detention pond to keep stormwater 
impoundment off of the UPRR 
embankment.  
• 
Utilizing the existing masonry arch 
structure, constructed in 1903, as a 
pedestrian underpass for the Greenway, 
thereby allowing public access to this 
historically significant structure. Utilizing 
this structure for pedestrians requires 
that new culverts be installed through the 
UPRR embankment for use as drainage 
conveyance.  The UPRR requires that any 
culvert / structure that passes under their 
tracks be hydraulically isolated from 
inundation of storm water during a 100-
year frequency storm event if it will be 
used as a pedestrian underpass. 
The first alternative kept the footprint of the 
proposed berm smaller, both within the Kiwanis Park land and including only a small portion within 
private property to the east.  The private property which would be impacted is located within the FEMA 
floodplain and acts as a retention pond currently.  This alternative did not provide enough stormwater 
storage area upstream of the UPRR tracks. It would require significantly more storm water to pass 
through the embankment to keep the limits of the floodplain upstream of Kiwanis Park to existing 
conditions.  Passing the additional stormwater through the embankment would require that this flow be 
Figure 2: 1903 Structure constructed for Drainage Conveyance 

 
East Park Greenway Underpass & Drainage Plan at the Union Pacific Railroad 
4 
 
mitigated by improvements made to the land and culverts downstream, including within the Laramie 
County Conservation District Managed LEADS property; under Campstool Road; through the Cheyenne 
Business Parkway, 8th Filing, Lot 2, Block 1; under Interstate 80; and potentially further downstream. 
The second, preferred, alternative will expand the berm into the adjacent two private properties to 
increase the Lower Dry Creek detention storage volume over that which was available in the first 
alternative.  The City of Cheyenne is currently pursuing the purchase of these two properties.  
Conclusion 
The Hydraulics and Hydrology study done by GLM Group concludes that construction of a berm within 
both Kiwanis Park and the adjacent properties, installation of drainage culverts through the UPRR 
embankment, and construction of a second berm and additional culvert to address stormwater coming 
to the site from east of Whitney Road, will hydraulically isolate the masonry arch structure such that it 
can be used as a pedestrian underpass.  This design meets the project goals of removing the buildup of 
storm water against the UPRR embankment that comes from the Lower Dry Creek Basin north of East 
Pershing Boulevard.  Additionally, the footprint of the floodplain, east of Whitney Road may be reduced 
following construction of the proposed improvements and the filing of a Letter of Map Revision with 
FEMA 
A Greenway has been conceptually designed around East Park to connect to the existing Greenway 
around Saddle Ridge Subdivision at the northeast corner of the intersection of East Pershing Boulevard 
and Whitney Road, as well as to the proposed Greenway envisioned to enter Kiwanis Park in the 
southwest corner as part of the Sun Valley Connector Greenway project.  The Greenway will utilize the 
masonry arch structure and continue through the Laramie County Conservation District Managed 
Cheyenne LEADS land to connect to the planned LEADS Trail along Campstool Road. 
Next Steps 
The implementation of this proposed design depends on the acceptance by the UPRR as it not only 
requires construction to take place within their right of way to construct the berm and to bore the 
proposed culverts through their embankment, but also to utilize the masonry arch structure as a 
pedestrian underpass.  To move forward the City of Cheyenne must enter into a Preliminary Engineering 
(PE) Agreement with the UPRR.  The PE Agreement will allow conceptual level plans, drainage report, 
geotechnical report, and culvert boring plans to be reviewed by UPRR or their subcontracted reviewers.  
A retainer must accompany the PE Agreement.  The cost of the retainer will be determined by the UPRR 
Manager of Public Projects based on the scope of work.  This particular project had a suggested retainer 
of $25,000 in 2021.  The UPRR will bill the City of Cheyenne for actual review costs, which could be in 
excess of the retainer.  Following acceptance of the conceptual plans the City of Cheyenne can proceed 
with final design plans, again to be reviewed by the UPRR, and ultimately construction if the plans are 
approved. 
We believe this conceptual plan is a win-win for both the City of Cheyenne and the Union Pacific 
Railroad.  Impoundment of stormwater on the UPRR embankment has reaches depths of 19 feet on 
multiple occasions in the past. A breach of the embankment in a large flood event would be catastrophic 
for downstream properties and the travelling public including traffic on Interstate 80, as well as 
catastrophic for the UPRR who relies on these mainline tracks for all railroad commerce into and out of 
Cheyenne to the east.
